Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I got really worked up in a staff meeting the other day. I'm a personal care aide, you know, and we were talking about fall prevention. And I just... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I definitely get like that. There are these stretches where I just feel... bright, you know? Really fired up, like everything in the world is finally lining up perfectly for me. I can get so much done. But the thing is, if someone slows me down or starts questioning what I'm doing, I just snap at them. Way harder than I mean to. 

Like my neighbor the other day, he was asking me why I was taking my lawnmower apart on the front porch. I was just cleaning the carburetor, which is actually super easy if you know what you're doing. Did you know small engines are basically the exact same design they were like fifty years ago? Briggs and Stratton makes almost all of them. My uncle worked for a tractor company back in the day, and he used to bring home these huge repair manuals. I love reading manuals. They have all these exploded diagrams showing every single washer and bolt. It's crazy how many little pieces go into a machine just to cut grass. I bet the ancient Egyptians would have loved a lawnmower, though they mostly had sand, right? 

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the energetic thing. But yeah, I get real fired up, but then I yell if people get in my way.

No, I don't really get like that. I don't get down or depressed for days or weeks. I mean, I might have a bad day here or there, but who doesn't? Like yesterday, they ran out of the good coffee in the breakroom at work, and I was pretty bummed about it. We usually get this dark roast, French vanilla kind. It's so much better than the regular stuff. Did you know coffee beans are actually the pits of a berry? They call them coffee cherries. I saw a show about it once. They have to pick them by hand, mostly, because they don't all ripen at the same time. It's wild to think about how much work goes into just one cup of coffee. I actually tried growing a coffee plant in my apartment once, but it needs way more humidity than I could give it. I ended up putting a plastic bag over it to make a little greenhouse, but then it got moldy. So yeah, I don't really get depressed or anything like that. Just regular bad days, you know?

Yeah, actually, when I get like that... when I'm sleeping maybe two, three hours a night and I have all these massive plans, I do get these... things. Like, I'll hear people calling my name, or like, shouting out orders, but there's nobody around. And I get this absolute, rock-solid feeling that my supervisor is setting traps for me at work. Which is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. Melatonin is a hormone, right? I tried taking those melatonin gummies once, the ones that taste like strawberry. They were pretty good, actually. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. 

But yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.

Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Like... there was a time, maybe a few months back, where I was actually pretty calm. I was sleeping okay, getting a normal amount of rest, not tearing apart lawnmowers or anything like that. But for a few weeks there, I was still hearing my name getting called out when nobody was around. And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. 

Which is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. 

But yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.

I bet the ancient Egyptians would have loved a lawnmower, though they mostly had sand, right? \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the energetic thing. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Just regular bad days, you know?\n\nDoctor: When you're in these energetic, fired-up periods, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice, or do you ever have beliefs that feel absolutely true to you even though people around you don't agree with them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, when I get like that... when I'm sleeping maybe two, three hours a night and I have all these massive plans, I do get these... things. Like, I'll hear people calling my name, or like, shouting out orders, but there's nobody around. And I get this absolute, rock-solid feeling that my supervisor is setting traps for me at work. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. Melatonin is a hormone, right? I tried taking those melatonin gummies once, the ones that taste like strawberry. They were pretty good, actually. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. \n\nBut yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices calling your name or when you feel certain that your supervisor is setting traps for you — do these things only happen during those times when you're feeling really energetic and fired up with only a few hours of sleep, or do they happen at other times too, even when you're not in one of those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Like... there was a time, maybe a few months back, where I was actually pretty calm. I was sleeping okay, getting a normal amount of rest, not tearing apart lawnmowers or anything like that. But for a few weeks there, I was still hearing my name getting called out when nobody was around. And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. \n\nWhich is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\n[Last Question]\nIn the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 41 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Additional Requirement 3\nmanifestation: \"There was a calmer stretch when I was sleeping more normally and wasn't rushing around with projects, but for several weeks I still heard my name being called and stayed convinced people at work were keeping notes on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I think I just said that, right? Like a few months ago, there was this stretch... maybe three or four weeks? I was sleeping fine, not doing the whole staying up all night tearing things apart routine. Just, you know, normal. But I was still hearing the voices calling out to me, and I was still totally sure they had those little notebooks at work and were writing down every little thing I did. 

Notebooks are funny, though. I used to have this Mead Five Star one when I was in high school. The wire spiral part always got caught on my sweater. I had this great green sweater... I think I left it on the bus one day. The buses around here are always running late anyway. Have you noticed that? You look at the schedule and it says 3:15, but it doesn't show up until 3:40, and by then it's raining and you don't have an umbrella. Umbrellas are one of those things you never buy until you actually need one, and then you have to buy it at the drugstore for like twenty bucks and it breaks the first time the wind blows. The wind was crazy last night, actually. It blew my neighbor's trash can all the way down the street. 

But yeah, like I was saying, there was that calm stretch where I wasn't all fired up, but I was still hearing my name and worrying about the notes.

present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Looking back, the high-energy spells with big plans and a short fuse have taken up more of this whole period than the calmer weeks have.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a calmer stretch when I was sleeping more normally and wasn't rushing around with projects, but for several weeks I still heard my name being called and stayed convinced people at work were keeping notes on me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I was put on leave from the senior center, and my wife has had to remind me about chores and appointments because I am not keeping up the way I used to. It has also made me pull back from church friends and worry whether I can safely return to caregiving work.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since the new supervisor started criticizing my work and taking away briefings I used to lead.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The suspicious thoughts and hearing calls that aren't there have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being too energized, sleeping very little, and pushing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 41 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S022\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: When you're in these energetic, fired-up periods, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice, or do you ever have beliefs that feel absolutely true to you even though people around you don't agree with them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, when I get like that... when I'm sleeping maybe two, three hours a night and I have all these massive plans, I do get these... things. Like, I'll hear people calling my name, or like, shouting out orders, but there's nobody around. And I get this absolute, rock-solid feeling that my supervisor is setting traps for me at work. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. Melatonin is a hormone, right? I tried taking those melatonin gummies once, the ones that taste like strawberry. They were pretty good, actually. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. \n\nBut yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices calling your name or when you feel certain that your supervisor is setting traps for you — do these things only happen during those times when you're feeling really energetic and fired up with only a few hours of sleep, or do they happen at other times too, even when you're not in one of those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Like... there was a time, maybe a few months back, where I was actually pretty calm. I was sleeping okay, getting a normal amount of rest, not tearing apart lawnmowers or anything like that. But for a few weeks there, I was still hearing my name getting called out when nobody was around. And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. \n\nWhich is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: In the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just said that, right? Like a few months ago, there was this stretch... maybe three or four weeks? I was sleeping fine, not doing the whole staying up all night tearing things apart routine. Just, you know, normal. But I was still hearing the voices calling out to me, and I was still totally sure they had those little notebooks at work and were writing down every little thing I did. \n\nNotebooks are funny, though. I used to have this Mead Five Star one when I was in high school. The wire spiral part always got caught on my sweater. I had this great green sweater... I think I left it on the bus one day. The buses around here are always running late anyway. Have you noticed that? You look at the schedule and it says 3:15, but it doesn't show up until 3:40, and by then it's raining and you don't have an umbrella. Umbrellas are one of those things you never buy until you actually need one, and then you have to buy it at the drugstore for like twenty bucks and it breaks the first time the wind blows. The wind was crazy last night, actually. It blew my neighbor's trash can all the way down the street. \n\nBut yeah, like I was saying, there was that calm stretch where I wasn't all fired up, but I was still hearing my name and worrying about the notes.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring that calm stretch a few months ago when you were hearing your name and worried about the notebooks — were you feeling depressed, sad, or down at all during those weeks, or were you just... neutral and normal mood-wise?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. No, I wasn't down in the dumps or anything like that. I wasn't sad at all. It was just a calmer stretch, you know? I was sleeping more normally, and I wasn't rushing around with all my projects. 

I usually have a lot of projects going on. Like, I was trying to take apart and fix this old toaster I found at a yard sale. Toasters are surprisingly complicated inside. I bite my tongue sometimes when I'm just chewing gum. I like the cinnamon kind, but the flavor goes away in like five minutes. It's a total rip-off. But yeah, my mood was just normal. I wasn't depressed. Just a calm stretch where I wasn't rushing around. But for several weeks there, I still heard my name being called out, and I stayed totally convinced that the people at work were keeping those notes on me.

I was just cleaning the carburetor, which is actually super easy if you know what you're doing. Did you know small engines are basically the exact same design they were like fifty years ago? Briggs and Stratton makes almost all of them. My uncle worked for a tractor company back in the day, and he used to bring home these huge repair manuals. I love reading manuals. They have all these exploded diagrams showing every single washer and bolt. It's crazy how many little pieces go into a machine just to cut grass. I bet the ancient Egyptians would have loved a lawnmower, though they mostly had sand, right? \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the energetic thing. But yeah, I get real fired up, but then I yell if people get in my way.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of those energetic, fired-up periods, do you find yourself feeling down, depressed, or really low for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. I don't get down or depressed for days or weeks. I mean, I might have a bad day here or there, but who doesn't? Like yesterday, they ran out of the good coffee in the breakroom at work, and I was pretty bummed about it. We usually get this dark roast, French vanilla kind. It's so much better than the regular stuff. Did you know coffee beans are actually the pits of a berry? They call them coffee cherries. I saw a show about it once. They have to pick them by hand, mostly, because they don't all ripen at the same time. It's wild to think about how much work goes into just one cup of coffee. I actually tried growing a coffee plant in my apartment once, but it needs way more humidity than I could give it. I ended up putting a plastic bag over it to make a little greenhouse, but then it got moldy. So yeah, I don't really get depressed or anything like that. Just regular bad days, you know?\n\nDoctor: When you're in these energetic, fired-up periods, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice, or do you ever have beliefs that feel absolutely true to you even though people around you don't agree with them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, when I get like that... when I'm sleeping maybe two, three hours a night and I have all these massive plans, I do get these... things. Like, I'll hear people calling my name, or like, shouting out orders, but there's nobody around. And I get this absolute, rock-solid feeling that my supervisor is setting traps for me at work. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. Melatonin is a hormone, right? I tried taking those melatonin gummies once, the ones that taste like strawberry. They were pretty good, actually. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. \n\nBut yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices calling your name or when you feel certain that your supervisor is setting traps for you — do these things only happen during those times when you're feeling really energetic and fired up with only a few hours of sleep, or do they happen at other times too, even when you're not in one of those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Like... there was a time, maybe a few months back, where I was actually pretty calm. I was sleeping okay, getting a normal amount of rest, not tearing apart lawnmowers or anything like that. But for a few weeks there, I was still hearing my name getting called out when nobody was around. And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. \n\nWhich is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: In the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just said that, right? The wind was crazy last night, actually. It blew my neighbor's trash can all the way down the street. \n\nBut yeah, like I was saying, there was that calm stretch where I wasn't all fired up, but I was still hearing my name and worrying about the notes.\n\nDoctor: During that calm stretch a few months ago when you were hearing your name and worried about the notebooks — were you feeling depressed, sad, or down at all during those weeks, or were you just... neutral and normal mood-wise?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't down in the dumps or anything like that. I wasn't sad at all. It was just a calmer stretch, you know? I was sleeping more normally, and I wasn't rushing around with all my projects. \n\nI usually have a lot of projects going on. They get bright orange when they heat up. Orange is a weird word, isn't it? People always say nothing rhymes with orange. Well, maybe door hinge, if you say it really fast. I think a rapper said that in an interview once. I wonder how those guys memorize all those words without getting tongue-tied. I bite my tongue sometimes when I'm just chewing gum. Most days, I'd say. Honestly, the suspicious thoughts and hearing those calls that aren't there... that's been going on for about nine months now. Nine months. That's a whole school year, basically. Or, you know, how long it takes to grow a baby. 

One of the nurses I work with just went on maternity leave. She was huge, like she swallowed a beach ball. Beach balls are so hard to inflate, you always get dizzy blowing them up because the little plastic valve keeps closing on you. You have to bite it with your teeth just to get the air in. Plastic tastes terrible. I read somewhere on the internet that we all eat like a credit card's worth of microplastics every year. Just from drinking water from bottles and stuff. It gets in the oceans, the fish eat it, and then people eat the fish. I don't really like fish anyway, it has too many tiny bones. Have you ever gotten a fish bone stuck in your throat? It's terrifying. You're supposed to eat a big piece of bread to push it down. Bread is pretty much good for everything, especially if you toast it. But not in that toaster I was trying to fix, obviously. 

But yeah, it's been about nine months of dealing with the voices and worrying about them keeping notes on me. Almost every day.

I was moving chairs around, waving my arms to show how you're supposed to catch someone, and I just couldn't seem to stop, even when folks were telling me to sit down. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. \n\nBut yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices calling your name or when you feel certain that your supervisor is setting traps for you — do these things only happen during those times when you're feeling really energetic and fired up with only a few hours of sleep, or do they happen at other times too, even when you're not in one of those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: In the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just said that, right? But I was still hearing the voices calling out to me, and I was still totally sure they had those little notebooks at work and were writing down every little thing I did. \n\nNotebooks are funny, though. I used to have this Mead Five Star one when I was in high school. The wire spiral part always got caught on my sweater. I had this great green sweater... I think I left it on the bus one day. The buses around here are always running late anyway. Have you noticed that? You look at the schedule and it says 3:15, but it doesn't show up until 3:40, and by then it's raining and you don't have an umbrella. Umbrellas are one of those things you never buy until you actually need one, and then you have to buy it at the drugstore for like twenty bucks and it breaks the first time the wind blows. The wind was crazy last night, actually. It blew my neighbor's trash can all the way down the street. \n\nBut yeah, like I was saying, there was that calm stretch where I wasn't all fired up, but I was still hearing my name and worrying about the notes.\n\nDoctor: During that calm stretch a few months ago when you were hearing your name and worried about the notebooks — were you feeling depressed, sad, or down at all during those weeks, or were you just... neutral and normal mood-wise?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't down in the dumps or anything like that. I wasn't sad at all. It was just a calmer stretch, you know? I was sleeping more normally, and I wasn't rushing around with all my projects. \n\nI usually have a lot of projects going on. Like, I was trying to take apart and fix this old toaster I found at a yard sale. Toasters are surprisingly complicated inside. All those little wire heating coils. They get bright orange when they heat up. Orange is a weird word, isn't it? People always say nothing rhymes with orange. Well, maybe door hinge, if you say it really fast. I think a rapper said that in an interview once. I wonder how those guys memorize all those words without getting tongue-tied. I bite my tongue sometimes when I'm just chewing gum. I like the cinnamon kind, but the flavor goes away in like five minutes. It's a total rip-off. You buy a whole pack and it's basically just spicy rubber after a few chews. \n\nBut yeah, my mood was just normal. I wasn't depressed. Just a calm stretch where I wasn't rushing around. Beach balls are so hard to inflate, you always get dizzy blowing them up because the little plastic valve keeps closing on you. You have to bite it with your teeth just to get the air in. Plastic tastes terrible. I read somewhere on the internet that we all eat like a credit card's worth of microplastics every year. Just from drinking water from bottles and stuff. Yeah, they definitely get stronger. When I was in those fired-up stretches, you know, when I was sleeping only a few hours and making all those big plans and taking things apart... I was also hearing those calls that weren't there. And I was totally sure my supervisor was setting traps for me. 

Traps are a weird concept, right? Like mouse traps. We had mice in our apartment when I was a kid, and my dad would set those wooden snap traps with the peanut butter. Peanut butter is way better than cheese for catching mice. Everybody thinks cheese because of cartoons. Cartoons always have mice eating Swiss cheese with the big holes in it. I always wondered how they get the holes in Swiss cheese. Like, do they poke them with a stick, or does it just grow that way? I used to love Tom and Jerry. They never really talked, just ran around hitting each other with frying pans. Frying pans are so heavy, especially the cast iron ones. You have to season them with oil or everything sticks. My mom had this one cast iron skillet she used for everything, even pancakes. Pancakes are great, but you need real maple syrup. That fake stuff is just corn syrup and brown food coloring. It comes in those plastic bottles shaped like a lady. I think her name is Aunt Jemima, but they changed it, didn't they? 

But yeah, when I'm all wired up and not sleeping, the voices and the feeling that my supervisor is out to get me with traps, it definitely gets way more intense.

But anyway, yeah, the meeting got a little out of hand, and my boss said I needed to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks or months, have you noticed periods where you felt unusually happy, energetic, or like you could do anything — and these feelings lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely get like that. There are these stretches where I just feel... bright, you know? Really fired up, like everything in the world is finally lining up perfectly for me. I can get so much done. But the thing is, if someone slows me down or starts questioning what I'm doing, I just snap at them. Way harder than I mean to. \n\nLike my neighbor the other day, he was asking me why I was taking my lawnmower apart on the front porch. I was just cleaning the carburetor, which is actually super easy if you know what you're doing. Did you know small engines are basically the exact same design they were like fifty years ago? Briggs and Stratton makes almost all of them. My uncle worked for a tractor company back in the day, and he used to bring home these huge repair manuals. I love reading manuals. They have all these exploded diagrams showing every single washer and bolt. It's crazy how many little pieces go into a machine just to cut grass. I bet the ancient Egyptians would have loved a lawnmower, though they mostly had sand, right? \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the energetic thing. But yeah, I get real fired up, but then I yell if people get in my way.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of those energetic, fired-up periods, do you find yourself feeling down, depressed, or really low for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. I don't get down or depressed for days or weeks. I mean, I might have a bad day here or there, but who doesn't? Like yesterday, they ran out of the good coffee in the breakroom at work, and I was pretty bummed about it. We usually get this dark roast, French vanilla kind. It's so much better than the regular stuff. Did you know coffee beans are actually the pits of a berry? They call them coffee cherries. I saw a show about it once. They have to pick them by hand, mostly, because they don't all ripen at the same time. It's wild to think about how much work goes into just one cup of coffee. I actually tried growing a coffee plant in my apartment once, but it needs way more humidity than I could give it. I ended up putting a plastic bag over it to make a little greenhouse, but then it got moldy. So yeah, I don't really get depressed or anything like that. Just regular bad days, you know?\n\nDoctor: When you're in these energetic, fired-up periods, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice, or do you ever have beliefs that feel absolutely true to you even though people around you don't agree with them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, when I get like that... when I'm sleeping maybe two, three hours a night and I have all these massive plans, I do get these... things. Like, I'll hear people calling my name, or like, shouting out orders, but there's nobody around. And I get this absolute, rock-solid feeling that my supervisor is setting traps for me at work. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. Melatonin is a hormone, right? I tried taking those melatonin gummies once, the ones that taste like strawberry. They were pretty good, actually. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. \n\nBut yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices calling your name or when you feel certain that your supervisor is setting traps for you — do these things only happen during those times when you're feeling really energetic and fired up with only a few hours of sleep, or do they happen at other times too, even when you're not in one of those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Like... there was a time, maybe a few months back, where I was actually pretty calm. I was sleeping okay, getting a normal amount of rest, not tearing apart lawnmowers or anything like that. But for a few weeks there, I was still hearing my name getting called out when nobody was around. And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. \n\nWhich is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: In the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just said that, right? Like a few months ago, there was this stretch... maybe three or four weeks? I was sleeping fine, not doing the whole staying up all night tearing things apart routine. Just, you know, normal. But I was still hearing the voices calling out to me, and I was still totally sure they had those little notebooks at work and were writing down every little thing I did. \n\nNotebooks are funny, though. I used to have this Mead Five Star one when I was in high school. The wire spiral part always got caught on my sweater. I had this great green sweater... I think I left it on the bus one day. The buses around here are always running late anyway. Have you noticed that? You look at the schedule and it says 3:15, but it doesn't show up until 3:40, and by then it's raining and you don't have an umbrella. Umbrellas are one of those things you never buy until you actually need one, and then you have to buy it at the drugstore for like twenty bucks and it breaks the first time the wind blows. The wind was crazy last night, actually. It blew my neighbor's trash can all the way down the street. \n\nBut yeah, like I was saying, there was that calm stretch where I wasn't all fired up, but I was still hearing my name and worrying about the notes.\n\nDoctor: During that calm stretch a few months ago when you were hearing your name and worried about the notebooks — were you feeling depressed, sad, or down at all during those weeks, or were you just... neutral and normal mood-wise?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't down in the dumps or anything like that. I wasn't sad at all. It was just a calmer stretch, you know? I was sleeping more normally, and I wasn't rushing around with all my projects. \n\nI usually have a lot of projects going on. Like, I was trying to take apart and fix this old toaster I found at a yard sale. Toasters are surprisingly complicated inside. All those little wire heating coils. They get bright orange when they heat up. Orange is a weird word, isn't it? People always say nothing rhymes with orange. Well, maybe door hinge, if you say it really fast. I think a rapper said that in an interview once. I wonder how those guys memorize all those words without getting tongue-tied. I bite my tongue sometimes when I'm just chewing gum. I like the cinnamon kind, but the flavor goes away in like five minutes. It's a total rip-off. You buy a whole pack and it's basically just spicy rubber after a few chews. \n\nBut yeah, my mood was just normal. I wasn't depressed. Just a calm stretch where I wasn't rushing around. But for several weeks there, I still heard my name being called out, and I stayed totally convinced that the people at work were keeping those notes on me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, setting aside those energetic periods where you're sleeping just a few hours — how often would you say you're hearing your name called out or feeling like people are writing notes about you? Is it happening most days, or just some days?\n\nPatient: Most days, I'd say. Honestly, the suspicious thoughts and hearing those calls that aren't there... that's been going on for about nine months now. Nine months. That's a whole school year, basically. Or, you know, how long it takes to grow a baby. \n\nOne of the nurses I work with just went on maternity leave. She was huge, like she swallowed a beach ball. Beach balls are so hard to inflate, you always get dizzy blowing them up because the little plastic valve keeps closing on you. You have to bite it with your teeth just to get the air in. Plastic tastes terrible. I read somewhere on the internet that we all eat like a credit card's worth of microplastics every year. Just from drinking water from bottles and stuff. It gets in the oceans, the fish eat it, and then people eat the fish. I don't really like fish anyway, it has too many tiny bones. Have you ever gotten a fish bone stuck in your throat? It's terrifying. You're supposed to eat a big piece of bread to push it down. Bread is pretty much good for everything, especially if you toast it. But not in that toaster I was trying to fix, obviously. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about nine months of dealing with the voices and worrying about them keeping notes on me. Almost every day.\n\nDoctor: When you're in those energetic, fired-up periods with just a few hours of sleep — do the voices and the beliefs about people watching you get much stronger and more intense than they are during your calmer stretches, or are they pretty much the same intensity all the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely get stronger. When I was in those fired-up stretches, you know, when I was sleeping only a few hours and making all those big plans and taking things apart... I was also hearing those calls that weren't there. And I was totally sure my supervisor was setting traps for me. \n\nTraps are a weird concept, right? Like mouse traps. We had mice in our apartment when I was a kid, and my dad would set those wooden snap traps with the peanut butter. Peanut butter is way better than cheese for catching mice. Everybody thinks cheese because of cartoons. Cartoons always have mice eating Swiss cheese with the big holes in it. I always wondered how they get the holes in Swiss cheese. Like, do they poke them with a stick, or does it just grow that way? I used to love Tom and Jerry. They never really talked, just ran around hitting each other with frying pans. Frying pans are so heavy, especially the cast iron ones. You have to season them with oil or everything sticks. My mom had this one cast iron skillet she used for everything, even pancakes. Pancakes are great, but you need real maple syrup. That fake stuff is just corn syrup and brown food coloring. It comes in those plastic bottles shaped like a lady. I think her name is Aunt Jemima, but they changed it, didn't they? \n\nBut yeah, when I'm all wired up and not sleeping, the voices and the feeling that my supervisor is out to get me with traps, it definitely gets way more intense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I think her name is Aunt Jemima, but they changed it, didn't they? \n\nBut yeah, when I'm all wired up and not sleeping, the voices and the feeling that my supervisor is out to get me with traps, it definitely gets way more intense.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring those calm stretches when you were sleeping normally and not feeling fired up — when you were still hearing your name called and worried about the notebooks — did you ever feel like those voices or beliefs went away completely for a few days, or were they pretty much constantly there the whole time?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 41 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Additional Requirement 3\nmanifestation: \"There was a calmer stretch when I was sleeping more normally and wasn't rushing around with projects, but for several weeks I still heard my name being called and stayed convinced people at work were keeping notes on me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They didn't really go away, no. Even during that calmer stretch, you know, when I was actually sleeping okay and not rushing around trying to fix things or start new projects... they were still there. For several weeks straight, I was still hearing my name being called out, and I stayed totally convinced that the people at work were keeping those notes on me. 

Notes are funny. Like sticky notes. Post-its. Did you know the guy who invented Post-its was actually trying to make a super strong adhesive, but he messed up and made a weak one instead? So they just used it for bookmarks that wouldn't fall out of hymnals at church. Church music is always so slow. I tried to learn the organ once when I was a kid. It has all those different keyboards layered on top of each other, and you have to play with your feet, too. It's like patting your head and rubbing your stomach at the same time. I can never do that. My uncle used to do this trick where it looked like he was pulling his thumb apart, but it was just his other thumb tucked underneath. He always smelled like peppermint and old cigars. 

But yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping normally, the voices calling my name and the worries about the notes didn't stop. They were pretty much constantly there.

And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. \n\nWhich is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: In the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just said that, right? Like a few months ago, there was this stretch... maybe three or four weeks? I was sleeping fine, not doing the whole staying up all night tearing things apart routine. Just, you know, normal. Yeah, my mood stays pretty normal during those times. Like I was just saying, there was that calmer stretch recently where I was sleeping more normally, you know? I wasn't rushing around with all those projects or anything. 

Projects... man, I used to love doing model airplanes when I was a kid. The glue smells so strong, though. It gets all over your fingers and dries like a second skin, and you spend hours just peeling it off. Peeling things is satisfying. Like when you get a bad sunburn. I got a terrible sunburn once at the beach in New Jersey. The sand gets everywhere, in your shoes, your car... cars are so expensive to fix now. The mechanics just plug a computer into it and charge you a hundred bucks just to tell you what the little light on the dashboard means. Dashboards used to just have regular dials. My granddad had this old truck that didn't even have a radio, just a hole where it was supposed to go. You had to sing to yourself if you wanted music. I can't sing at all, my voice sounds like a rusty hinge. 

Anyway, what was I... oh, right, my mood. Yeah, it didn't really shift or change. I was calm. But even though my mood was totally stable for those several weeks, I still heard my name being called out, and I stayed completely convinced the people at work were keeping those notes on me. I'm Dr. [Name], a psychiatrist. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is... I got really worked up in a staff meeting the other day. I'm a personal care aide, you know, and we were talking about fall prevention. And I just... I started acting out this whole fall-prevention drill right there in the room. I was moving chairs around, waving my arms to show how you're supposed to catch someone, and I just couldn't seem to stop, even when folks were telling me to sit down. It was like I had to show them exactly how it works. Which reminds me, I saw this documentary on TV last night about the history of chairs. Did you know the ancient Egyptians had chairs with animal legs carved into them? It's fascinating, really, how furniture has evolved. But anyway, yeah, the meeting got a little out of hand, and my boss said I needed to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks or months, have you noticed periods where you felt unusually happy, energetic, or like you could do anything — and these feelings lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely get like that. There are these stretches where I just feel... bright, you know? Really fired up, like everything in the world is finally lining up perfectly for me. I can get so much done. But the thing is, if someone slows me down or starts questioning what I'm doing, I just snap at them. Way harder than I mean to. \n\nLike my neighbor the other day, he was asking me why I was taking my lawnmower apart on the front porch. I was just cleaning the carburetor, which is actually super easy if you know what you're doing. Did you know small engines are basically the exact same design they were like fifty years ago? Briggs and Stratton makes almost all of them. My uncle worked for a tractor company back in the day, and he used to bring home these huge repair manuals. I love reading manuals. They have all these exploded diagrams showing every single washer and bolt. It's crazy how many little pieces go into a machine just to cut grass. I bet the ancient Egyptians would have loved a lawnmower, though they mostly had sand, right? \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the energetic thing. But yeah, I get real fired up, but then I yell if people get in my way.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of those energetic, fired-up periods, do you find yourself feeling down, depressed, or really low for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really get like that. I don't get down or depressed for days or weeks. I mean, I might have a bad day here or there, but who doesn't? Like yesterday, they ran out of the good coffee in the breakroom at work, and I was pretty bummed about it. We usually get this dark roast, French vanilla kind. It's so much better than the regular stuff. Did you know coffee beans are actually the pits of a berry? They call them coffee cherries. I saw a show about it once. They have to pick them by hand, mostly, because they don't all ripen at the same time. It's wild to think about how much work goes into just one cup of coffee. I actually tried growing a coffee plant in my apartment once, but it needs way more humidity than I could give it. I ended up putting a plastic bag over it to make a little greenhouse, but then it got moldy. So yeah, I don't really get depressed or anything like that. Just regular bad days, you know?\n\nDoctor: When you're in these energetic, fired-up periods, do you ever hear voices or see things that other people don't seem to notice, or do you ever have beliefs that feel absolutely true to you even though people around you don't agree with them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, when I get like that... when I'm sleeping maybe two, three hours a night and I have all these massive plans, I do get these... things. Like, I'll hear people calling my name, or like, shouting out orders, but there's nobody around. And I get this absolute, rock-solid feeling that my supervisor is setting traps for me at work. Like she's intentionally moving the wheelchairs to places where I'll trip over them, or messing with the schedule to make me look bad. \n\nWhich is crazy, because she usually works the day shift, and I'm mostly evenings. Did you know that working evening shifts actually changes your circadian rhythm? I read this article about how night shift workers have a higher risk for all sorts of health stuff because their bodies think it's still daytime. It has to do with melatonin. Melatonin is a hormone, right? I tried taking those melatonin gummies once, the ones that taste like strawberry. They were pretty good, actually. I ended up eating like five of them because they tasted like candy, and I had the weirdest dreams. I dreamt I was riding a giant lawnmower through a shopping mall. Malls are so dead these days, aren't they? All the ones near me are just full of empty storefronts and maybe one pretzel place. I love those soft pretzels with the big grains of salt on them. \n\nBut yeah, I hear the calls and I just know she's setting traps for me when I'm all fired up like that.\n\nDoctor: When you hear those voices calling your name or when you feel certain that your supervisor is setting traps for you — do these things only happen during those times when you're feeling really energetic and fired up with only a few hours of sleep, or do they happen at other times too, even when you're not in one of those high-energy periods?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's not just when I'm super energetic. Like... there was a time, maybe a few months back, where I was actually pretty calm. I was sleeping okay, getting a normal amount of rest, not tearing apart lawnmowers or anything like that. But for a few weeks there, I was still hearing my name getting called out when nobody was around. And I was totally convinced that the people at work were keeping little notebooks on me, writing down everything I did wrong. \n\nWhich is ridiculous, because who has time to write things down anymore? Everyone just uses their phones. I see the younger aides at work constantly on their phones. They have these pop-socket things on the back so they don't drop them. I tried one of those once, but it got stuck in my pocket. My pockets are always full of stuff anyway—keys, pens, those little alcohol prep pads. You know those prep pads dry out really fast if you don't seal them right? I learned that the hard way when I went to clean a scrape on my arm and it was just a dry piece of paper. Paper is made from trees, obviously, but did you know they can make paper out of hemp, too? George Washington grew hemp. Or was it Thomas Jefferson? One of those guys. They had huge farms back then. Farming seems like it would be peaceful, just you and the dirt. \n\nBut yeah, even when I was calm and sleeping fine, I still heard the voices and thought they were keeping notes on me.\n\nDoctor: In the past several months, have there been stretches of time — maybe a week or two or longer — where you were feeling pretty normal, not particularly energetic or fired up, sleeping okay, but you were still hearing those voices or still had those beliefs about people watching you or writing things down about you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just said that, right? Like a few months ago, there was this stretch... maybe three or four weeks? I was sleeping fine, not doing the whole staying up all night tearing things apart routine. Just, you know, normal. But I was still hearing the voices calling out to me, and I was still totally sure they had those little notebooks at work and were writing down every little thing I did. \n\nNotebooks are funny, though. I used to have this Mead Five Star one when I was in high school. The wire spiral part always got caught on my sweater. I had this great green sweater... I think I left it on the bus one day. The buses around here are always running late anyway. Have you noticed that? You look at the schedule and it says 3:15, but it doesn't show up until 3:40, and by then it's raining and you don't have an umbrella. Umbrellas are one of those things you never buy until you actually need one, and then you have to buy it at the drugstore for like twenty bucks and it breaks the first time the wind blows. The wind was crazy last night, actually. It blew my neighbor's trash can all the way down the street. \n\nBut yeah, like I was saying, there was that calm stretch where I wasn't all fired up, but I was still hearing my name and worrying about the notes.\n\nDoctor: During that calm stretch a few months ago when you were hearing your name and worried about the notebooks — were you feeling depressed, sad, or down at all during those weeks, or were you just... neutral and normal mood-wise?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't down in the dumps or anything like that. I wasn't sad at all. It was just a calmer stretch, you know? I was sleeping more normally, and I wasn't rushing around with all my projects. \n\nI usually have a lot of projects going on. Like, I was trying to take apart and fix this old toaster I found at a yard sale. Toasters are surprisingly complicated inside. All those little wire heating coils. They get bright orange when they heat up. Orange is a weird word, isn't it? People always say nothing rhymes with orange. Well, maybe door hinge, if you say it really fast. I think a rapper said that in an interview once. I wonder how those guys memorize all those words without getting tongue-tied. I bite my tongue sometimes when I'm just chewing gum. I like the cinnamon kind, but the flavor goes away in like five minutes. It's a total rip-off. You buy a whole pack and it's basically just spicy rubber after a few chews. \n\nBut yeah, my mood was just normal. I wasn't depressed. Just a calm stretch where I wasn't rushing around. But for several weeks there, I still heard my name being called out, and I stayed totally convinced that the people at work were keeping those notes on me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, setting aside those energetic periods where you're sleeping just a few hours — how often would you say you're hearing your name called out or feeling like people are writing notes about you? Is it happening most days, or just some days?\n\nPatient: Most days, I'd say. Honestly, the suspicious thoughts and hearing those calls that aren't there... that's been going on for about nine months now. Nine months. That's a whole school year, basically. Or, you know, how long it takes to grow a baby. \n\nOne of the nurses I work with just went on maternity leave. She was huge, like she swallowed a beach ball. Beach balls are so hard to inflate, you always get dizzy blowing them up because the little plastic valve keeps closing on you. You have to bite it with your teeth just to get the air in. Plastic tastes terrible. I read somewhere on the internet that we all eat like a credit card's worth of microplastics every year. Just from drinking water from bottles and stuff. It gets in the oceans, the fish eat it, and then people eat the fish. I don't really like fish anyway, it has too many tiny bones. Have you ever gotten a fish bone stuck in your throat? It's terrifying. You're supposed to eat a big piece of bread to push it down. Bread is pretty much good for everything, especially if you toast it. But not in that toaster I was trying to fix, obviously. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about nine months of dealing with the voices and worrying about them keeping notes on me. Almost every day.\n\nDoctor: When you're in those energetic, fired-up periods with just a few hours of sleep — do the voices and the beliefs about people watching you get much stronger and more intense than they are during your calmer stretches, or are they pretty much the same intensity all the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely get stronger. When I was in those fired-up stretches, you know, when I was sleeping only a few hours and making all those big plans and taking things apart... I was also hearing those calls that weren't there. And I was totally sure my supervisor was setting traps for me. \n\nTraps are a weird concept, right? Like mouse traps. We had mice in our apartment when I was a kid, and my dad would set those wooden snap traps with the peanut butter. Peanut butter is way better than cheese for catching mice. Everybody thinks cheese because of cartoons. Cartoons always have mice eating Swiss cheese with the big holes in it. I always wondered how they get the holes in Swiss cheese. Like, do they poke them with a stick, or does it just grow that way? I used to love Tom and Jerry. They never really talked, just ran around hitting each other with frying pans. Frying pans are so heavy, especially the cast iron ones. You have to season them with oil or everything sticks. My mom had this one cast iron skillet she used for everything, even pancakes. Pancakes are great, but you need real maple syrup. That fake stuff is just corn syrup and brown food coloring. It comes in those plastic bottles shaped like a lady. I can never do that. My uncle used to do this trick where it looked like he was pulling his thumb apart, but it was just his other thumb tucked underneath. I got a terrible sunburn once at the beach in New Jersey. The sand gets everywhere, in your shoes, your car... cars are so expensive to fix now. The psychotic symptoms are present most days regardless of mood stability or energy level, with intensification during manic-like episodes. This pattern is consistent with F20.0 (paranoid type) rather than F20.2, and rules out bipolar disorder with psychotic features (where psychosis would be primarily mood-congruent and episodic). ```json
{
  "diagnosis": "F20.0",
  "candidates": ["F20.0", "F25.0"],
  "reason": "Patient presents with a 9-month history of persistent psychotic symptoms (auditory hallucinations and delusions of reference) that occur independently of mood episodes. While the patient experiences distinct periods of elevated mood with decreased need for sleep, goal-directed activity, and irritability (consistent with manic/hypomanic episodes), the critical diagnostic feature is that psychotic symptoms persist during euthymic periods with normal sleep and stable mood. During a documented 3-4 week calm period with normal sleep and neutral mood, the patient continued to hear voices calling their name and maintained fixed beliefs about coworkers keeping notebooks on them.
